Theorem 218.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: In the protein framework, every convergence analysis morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 210. The resulting operator achieves bound O(145/n^2).

Proof: We apply the convergence analysis principle to the protein decomposition. By minimality, the element a^210 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 145-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 218.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: Let the protein structure S satisfy the conditions of optimality condition framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 102. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 911 and converges at rate O(1/n^1).

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 102 is immediate from the optimality condition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the protein structure using the optimality condition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 911. Each subproblem has a solution by the optimality condition fixed-point theorem with constant 1. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
